### Building-Block Aware Generative Modeling for 3D Crystals of Metal Organic Frameworks
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.08531v1)
- **Authors**: Chenru Duan, Aditya Nandy, Sizhan Liu, Yuanqi Du, Liu He, Yi Qu, Haojun Jia, Jin-Hu Dou
- **Abstract**: Metal-organic frameworks (MOFs) marry inorganic nodes, organic edges, and topological nets into programmable porous crystals, yet their astronomical design space defies brute-force synthesis. Generative modeling holds ultimate promise, but existing models either recycle known building blocks or are restricted to small unit cells. We introduce Building-Block-Aware MOF Diffusion (BBA MOF Diffusion), an SE(3)-equivariant diffusion model that learns 3D all-atom representations of individual building blocks, encoding crystallographic topological nets explicitly. Trained on the CoRE-MOF database, BBA MOF Diffusion readily samples MOFs with unit cells containing 1000 atoms with great geometric validity, novelty, and diversity mirroring experimental databases. Its native building-block representation produces unprecedented metal nodes and organic edges, expanding accessible chemical space by orders of magnitude. One high-scoring [Zn(1,4-TDC)(EtOH)2] MOF predicted by the model was synthesized, where powder X-ray diffraction, thermogravimetric analysis, and N2 sorption confirm its structural fidelity. BBA-Diff thus furnishes a practical pathway to synthesizable and high-performing MOFs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Building-Block-Aware MOF Diffusion (BBA MOF Diffusion), a novel SE(3)-equivariant diffusion model designed for generating 3D crystals of metal-organic frameworks (MOFs). Unlike previous generative models, BBA MOF Diffusion learns 3D all-atom representations of individual building blocks (nodes, edges, topological nets) and explicitly encodes crystallographic topological nets. The model is trained on the CoRE-MOF database and demonstrates the ability to sample MOFs with large unit cells (up to 1000 atoms) exhibiting geometric validity, novelty, and diversity comparable to experimental databases. A key strength is the model's ability to generate novel metal nodes and organic edges, expanding the accessible chemical space.  The paper validates the model's predictions through the synthesis of a predicted MOF, [Zn(1,4 – TDC)(EtOH)2], confirming its structural fidelity using powder X-ray diffraction, thermogravimetric analysis, and N2 sorption.  The authors argue that BBA MOF Diffusion provides a practical pathway to discovering synthesizable and high-performing MOFs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper demonstrates a significant advance over existing MOF generative models. While previous models often recycled known building blocks or were limited to small unit cells, BBA MOF Diffusion innovates by learning 3D all-atom representations of building blocks and incorporating topological net information. This approach enables the generation of novel nodes and edges, expanding the chemical space for MOF design. Furthermore, the synthesis of a MOF using the model suggests the model can make predictions which have some merit.

*   **Significance:** MOFs are a critical area of research for materials science with implications for energy storage, catalysis, and separation. The ability to computationally design and discover MOFs using generative models could significantly accelerate the development of new materials with tailored properties. While many models exist to generate MOFs, the size of the potential chemical space, and the computational limitations associated with exploring this space make this particular field a worthwhile area of continued research.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Building-Block Representation:** The core strength lies in the building-block approach, enabling the generation of novel nodes and edges.
    *   **Scalability:** The model's ability to handle large unit cells is a major advantage over previous approaches.
    *   **Experimental Validation:**  The synthesis and characterization of a predicted MOF provides strong evidence for the model's accuracy and potential for real-world application.
    *   **Clear and Well-Structured:** The paper is well-written, presenting the methodology and results in a clear and logical manner.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Topological Nets:** The model is trained on only four simple topological nets, restricting its ability to generate MOFs with entirely novel topologies.  While the authors argue this is representative of common synthesis efforts, a more diverse training set would enhance the model's versatility.
    *   **Focus on Single Node/Edge MOFs:** The current model is restricted to MOFs with a single inorganic node and organic edge. This limits the structural complexity that can be generated.
    *   **No Property-Driven Generation:**  The model does not directly incorporate property prediction into the generation process. While structural validity is important, the ultimate goal is to design MOFs with specific functions, and this capability is currently lacking.

*   **Potential Impact:** BBA MOF Diffusion has the potential to significantly impact MOF design by providing a powerful tool for exploring a wider range of chemical space and accelerating the discovery of novel materials. The model's scalability and ability to generate novel building blocks make it a promising approach for addressing the challenges in MOF design. More work should be done to explore the property space offered by this model, and to address model diversity.

**Overall Score:** 7

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a novel and well-executed approach to MOF generation with a significant improvement in scalability. The experimental validation is a critical strength. However, limitations in the topological diversity of the training data and the lack of property-driven design capabilities prevent it from achieving a higher score. The work is likely to be highly influential in the field, inspiring further research in building-block-based generative modeling for MOFs. Future research should prioritize expanding the model's capability to generate more complex architectures and directly target desired properties.

### The Truth Becomes Clearer Through Debate! Multi-Agent Systems with Large Language Models Unmask Fake News
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.08532v1)
- **Authors**: Yuhan Liu, Yuxuan Liu, Xiaoqing Zhang, Xiuying Chen, Rui Yan
- **Abstract**: In today's digital environment, the rapid propagation of fake news via social networks poses significant social challenges. Most existing detection methods either employ traditional classification models, which suffer from low interpretability and limited generalization capabilities, or craft specific prompts for large language models (LLMs) to produce explanations and results directly, failing to leverage LLMs' reasoning abilities fully. Inspired by the saying that "truth becomes clearer through debate," our study introduces a novel multi-agent system with LLMs named TruEDebate (TED) to enhance the interpretability and effectiveness of fake news detection. TED employs a rigorous debate process inspired by formal debate settings. Central to our approach are two innovative components: the DebateFlow Agents and the InsightFlow Agents. The DebateFlow Agents organize agents into two teams, where one supports and the other challenges the truth of the news. These agents engage in opening statements, cross-examination, rebuttal, and closing statements, simulating a rigorous debate process akin to human discourse analysis, allowing for a thorough evaluation of news content. Concurrently, the InsightFlow Agents consist of two specialized sub-agents: the Synthesis Agent and the Analysis Agent. The Synthesis Agent summarizes the debates and provides an overarching viewpoint, ensuring a coherent and comprehensive evaluation. The Analysis Agent, which includes a role-aware encoder and a debate graph, integrates role embeddings and models the interactions between debate roles and arguments using an attention mechanism, providing the final judgment.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces TruEDebate (TED), a novel multi-agent system leveraging Large Language Models (LLMs) for fake news detection. TED is inspired by the idea that truth emerges through debate and simulates a formal debate setting with agents organized into proponent and opponent teams. These agents engage in opening statements, cross-examination, rebuttals, and closing statements.  The system incorporates DebateFlow Agents that manage the debate and InsightFlow Agents, which include a Synthesis Agent to summarize the debate and an Analysis Agent to provide a final judgment based on a role-aware encoder and a debate graph. The paper presents experimental results on the ARG-EN and ARG-CN datasets, demonstrating TED's superior performance compared to traditional and LLM-based methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of using a structured debate framework for fake news detection is relatively novel. Prior work has explored multi-agent systems and LLMs for this task, but TED's formal debate structure (inspired by Lincoln-Douglas debate) and specific components (DebateFlow and InsightFlow Agents with role-aware encoding) provide a unique approach. The use of debate graphs to model interactions is also a valuable contribution.  However, multi-agent simulation, using roles for enhanced reasoning, and integrating LLMs in agents are not entirely new, requiring careful differentiation.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a crucial problem: the proliferation of fake news and the limitations of existing detection methods. The focus on interpretability is particularly important, as many existing approaches are black boxes.  TED attempts to enhance interpretability by making the reasoning process transparent through the debate structure and the debate report.  The performance improvements reported over baselines are promising. The release of the code is also a valuable contribution, enabling further research and potential real-world applications.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Motivation:** The paper clearly articulates the problem and motivates the need for a more interpretable and effective fake news detection method.
    *   **Novel Framework:** The TED framework provides a novel and well-defined approach to fake news detection.
    *   **Structured Debate Process:**  The formal debate structure ensures a comprehensive evaluation of news content from multiple perspectives.
    *   **Role-aware Encoder and Debate Graph:** The integration of role embeddings and debate graphs in the Analysis Agent is a significant contribution, capturing the dynamics of the debate process.
    *   **Strong Experimental Results:** The experimental results on two datasets demonstrate TED's superior performance compared to existing methods.
    *   **Code Release:** The authors have released their code, which facilitates reproducibility and encourages further research in this area.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Computational Cost:** Simulating a debate involving multiple LLM-powered agents can be computationally expensive and time-consuming. The paper doesn't adequately address the efficiency of the TED framework.
    *   **Prompt Engineering Dependency:** The performance of TED relies heavily on the quality of the prompts used for argumentation, cross-examination, rebuttal, and closing statements.  The paper does not provide exhaustive details about prompt optimization, which could affect the reproducibility and generalizability of the results.
    *   **Subjectivity in Debate:** Despite the formal structure, the debate process is inherently subjective, as the LLM agents may have biases or limitations that affect the quality of their arguments and rebuttals.  The paper needs to address potential mitigation strategies for this subjectivity.
    *   **Limited Scope of Analysis:** While the debate report provides some level of interpretability, it may not be sufficient to fully understand the complex reasoning process of the LLM agents.  The paper could explore additional methods for analyzing the debate dynamics and identifying key factors that influence the final decision.
     * **Comparative Depth:** The analysis of the differences between the closed-source and open-source models, in terms of qualitative performance and underlying factors (reasoning capabilities), requires further exploration.

*   **Potential Influence:**  TED has the potential to influence the field of fake news detection by introducing a more interpretable and effective approach. It could inspire the development of new multi-agent systems and debate-based frameworks for addressing other complex problems.  The role-aware encoding and debate graph techniques could also be applied in other areas of natural language processing.

**Justification of Score:**

Considering the novelty, significance, strengths, and weaknesses of the paper, a score of **7.5** is justified. The paper presents a valuable contribution to the field of fake news detection by introducing the TED framework, which integrates LLMs into a structured debate setting. The experimental results demonstrate TED's superior performance compared to existing methods, and the focus on interpretability is a significant advantage. However, the paper also has some limitations, including the computational cost, prompt engineering dependency, subjectivity in debate, and limited scope of analysis. While these limitations don't diminish the overall value of the paper, they prevent it from receiving a higher score. The score highlights the promise of the approach and also the areas where future research could further solidify the contribution and its impact.

Score: 7.5

- **Score**: 7/10

### Diffusion-assisted Model Predictive Control Optimization for Power System Real-Time Operation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.08535v1)
- **Authors**: Linna Xu, Yongli Zhu
- **Abstract**: This paper presents a modified model predictive control (MPC) framework for real-time power system operation. The framework incorporates a diffusion model tailored for time series generation to enhance the accuracy of the load forecasting module used in the system operation. In the absence of explicit state transition law, a model-identification procedure is leveraged to derive the system dynamics, thereby eliminating a barrier when applying MPC to a renewables-dominated power system. Case study results on an industry park system and the IEEE 30-bus system demonstrate that using the diffusion model to augment the training dataset significantly improves load-forecasting accuracy, and the inferred system dynamics are applicable to the real-time grid operation with solar and wind.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will summarize the paper and provide a critical evaluation of its novelty and significance, along with a justified score.

**Summary:**

The paper proposes a modified model predictive control (MPC) framework tailored for real-time power system operation. The key innovations lie in: (1) incorporating a time-series diffusion model (TS-Diffusion) to augment limited load data for more accurate load forecasting and (2) using a system identification approach to infer the unknown state transition dynamics required by the MPC framework, especially in the presence of renewable energy sources. The paper demonstrates the effectiveness of this approach through case studies on an industry park system and the IEEE 30-bus system, showing improved load forecasting accuracy and lower operational costs compared to benchmark approaches. The use of both static and dynamic estimation of the state transfer matrices is considered.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The paper presents a combination of existing techniques (MPC, diffusion models, system identification) but integrates them in a novel way for power system operation. The use of TS-Diffusion for data augmentation in the context of MPC for power systems appears to be a novel application. Further, while system identification is a well-established field, the specific application to inferring the state transition matrices in an MPC framework for renewable-dominated power systems, where explicit state transition laws are often unavailable, contributes incremental novelty. The method of dynamic inference is a significant novelty.

**Significance:**

The paper addresses a practical and important problem: the operation of power systems with high penetration of renewables, where uncertainties and the lack of explicit system dynamics pose challenges to traditional MPC approaches. Enhancing load forecasting accuracy and enabling MPC in the absence of explicit system models are valuable contributions to the field. The case studies provide empirical evidence of the effectiveness of the proposed approach, suggesting its potential for real-world applications.
* The significance is limited to the system sizes it is demonstrated on. It is unlikely that this would scale well to significantly larger networks.
* In the case studies, only an IEEE 30-bus system and an industrial park are used.

**Strengths:**

*   **Addresses a practical problem:** The paper targets the challenges of integrating renewable energy sources into power systems using MPC.
*   **Novel combination of techniques:** It integrates TS-Diffusion for data augmentation with system identification and MPC in a power systems context.
*   **Empirical validation:** The case studies demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed approach in reducing operating costs and improving load forecasting accuracy.
*   **Clear presentation:** The paper is well-written and clearly explains the proposed methodology.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited novelty in individual components:** The individual techniques (MPC, diffusion models, system identification) are not novel in themselves; the novelty lies primarily in their integration.
*   **Complexity:** The integration of multiple techniques adds complexity to the overall framework, potentially making it more difficult to implement and maintain.
*   **Scalability:** While the case studies demonstrate the effectiveness of the approach on a relatively small system, it is not clear how well it would scale to larger, more complex power systems. *This is a notable concern*
*   **Limited real-world validation:** The case studies are based on simulated data; real-world validation would further strengthen the paper's claims.
*   **Hyperparameter tuning:** The paper does not discuss the sensitivity of the results to the hyperparameters of the TS-Diffusion model or the system identification procedure.

**Potential Influence:**

The paper could influence future research in the area of MPC for power systems, particularly in the context of renewable energy integration. The approach of using diffusion models for data augmentation and system identification to enable MPC in the absence of explicit system dynamics could inspire further exploration and development in this area. It may allow utilities to deploy advanced MPC solutions on existing hardware, with less effort needed for system identification.

**Score Justification:**

I assign a score of **6.5** to this paper. While the individual components are not groundbreaking, the novel integration of TS-Diffusion for data augmentation and system identification to facilitate MPC in renewable-dominated power systems presents a valuable and practically relevant contribution. The case studies provide empirical support for the effectiveness of the approach. However, the limitations related to scalability, real-world validation, and hyperparameter sensitivity need to be addressed in future research. The modest impact on the field is reflected by the score.

### Guiding LLM-based Smart Contract Generation with Finite State Machine
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.08542v1)
- **Authors**: Hao Luo, Yuhao Lin, Xiao Yan, Xintong Hu, Yuxiang Wang, Qiming Zeng, Hao Wang, Jiawei Jiang
- **Abstract**: Smart contract is a kind of self-executing code based on blockchain technology with a wide range of application scenarios, but the traditional generation method relies on manual coding and expert auditing, which has a high threshold and low efficiency. Although Large Language Models (LLMs) show great potential in programming tasks, they still face challenges in smart contract generation w.r.t. effectiveness and security. To solve these problems, we propose FSM-SCG, a smart contract generation framework based on finite state machine (FSM) and LLMs, which significantly improves the quality of the generated code by abstracting user requirements to generate FSM, guiding LLMs to generate smart contracts, and iteratively optimizing the code with the feedback of compilation and security checks. The experimental results show that FSM-SCG significantly improves the quality of smart contract generation. Compared to the best baseline, FSM-SCG improves the compilation success rate of generated smart contract code by at most 48%, and reduces the average vulnerability risk score by approximately 68%.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces FSM-SCG, a framework designed to enhance smart contract generation using Large Language Models (LLMs). It addresses the challenges of low compilation rates and security vulnerabilities often found in LLM-generated smart contracts. FSM-SCG leverages Finite State Machines (FSMs) to abstract user requirements, guides LLMs to generate code based on these FSMs, and iteratively refines the code using compilation and security checks.  The approach follows a requirement-to-FSM-to-code (R2F2C) pattern.  Experiments show that FSM-SCG significantly improves compilation success rates and reduces vulnerability risk scores compared to direct generation and methods employing other intermediate representations. The paper also contributes a new fine-tuning dataset for LLMs in this domain.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the systematic integration of FSMs as an intermediate representation specifically tailored for LLM-based smart contract generation, coupled with an iterative refinement process based on compilation and security feedback. While prior work explores LLMs for smart contracts and uses intermediate representations, the specific choice of FSMs, the way they are integrated into the LLM prompting and fine-tuning process, and the comprehensive feedback loop distinguishes this work. The SmartFSM extension to the Mealy machine, although not dramatically different, also contributes a small increment of novelty.

*   **Significance:** The significance stems from directly addressing the practical limitations of using LLMs to generate secure and functional smart contracts. By significantly improving compilation rates and reducing vulnerabilities, FSM-SCG moves closer to enabling the automated generation of reliable blockchain code. The open-source fine-tuning dataset is also a valuable contribution that can facilitate further research in this area.  The paper offers a practical methodology to improve LLM generated smart contracts, an area of increasing importance.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Clear problem definition: The paper clearly identifies the shortcomings of current LLM-based smart contract generation.
    *   Well-defined methodology: FSM-SCG is a well-structured framework with clear steps.
    *   Empirical validation:  The experiments are thorough, comparing against several baselines and using multiple LLMs.  Ablation studies provide insights into the contribution of different components.
    *   Practical Relevance: The demonstrated improvements in compilation and security directly address real-world concerns in smart contract development.
    *   Reproducibility: The release of the fine-tuning dataset enhances the reproducibility and comparability of future work.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   Limited Generalizability claims: While the experiments demonstrate improvements, the results may not be generalizable to all types of smart contracts or different blockchain platforms without further validation. The experiments are also limited to one round of compilation feedback and one round of security feedback. Multiple rounds may improve the success rate further.
    *   SmartFSM contribution: The SmartFSM, while helpful, is an incremental improvement over Mealy machines. The impact of this specific design choice isn't fully isolated and rigorously evaluated. The improvement may stem more from the overall framework rather than just the SmartFSM itself.
    *   LLM selection bias: The study's reliance on specific LLMs (e.g., Llama and Qwen) may limit the generalizability of the findings to all LLMs. It's unclear if similar improvements would be observed with other architectures or fine-tuning approaches.
    *   Qualitative analysis: the qualitative analysis of the improved generation from a security standpoint could be further expanded.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the development of more robust and reliable smart contract generation tools. Other researchers can build upon this framework by exploring different intermediate representations, refinement strategies, and LLM architectures. The open-source dataset will also promote further research and benchmarking in the field.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper offers a sound methodology with empirical evidence to support its claims, along with an important contribution to the fine-tuning dataset availability. While some limitations exist concerning the generalizability, the novel integration of FSMs and feedback loop, along with the practical relevance and potential for impact, warrants a good score. The SmartFSM is an incremental contribution. The core idea is valuable, but the implementation could be better and it still depends heavily on LLMs which is not ideal for verifiable smart contracts.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### Small but Significant: On the Promise of Small Language Models for Accessible AIED
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.08588v1)
- **Authors**: Yumou Wei, Paulo Carvalho, John Stamper
- **Abstract**: GPT has become nearly synonymous with large language models (LLMs), an increasingly popular term in AIED proceedings. A simple keyword-based search reveals that 61% of the 76 long and short papers presented at AIED 2024 describe novel solutions using LLMs to address some of the long-standing challenges in education, and 43% specifically mention GPT. Although LLMs pioneered by GPT create exciting opportunities to strengthen the impact of AI on education, we argue that the field's predominant focus on GPT and other resource-intensive LLMs (with more than 10B parameters) risks neglecting the potential impact that small language models (SLMs) can make in providing resource-constrained institutions with equitable and affordable access to high-quality AI tools. Supported by positive results on knowledge component (KC) discovery, a critical challenge in AIED, we demonstrate that SLMs such as Phi-2 can produce an effective solution without elaborate prompting strategies. Hence, we call for more attention to developing SLM-based AIED approaches.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper argues that the AIED (Artificial Intelligence in Education) community's current overwhelming focus on large language models (LLMs) like GPT is overshadowing the potential of small language models (SLMs) to provide more accessible and equitable AIED solutions.  The authors point out the resource constraints many educational institutions face, making LLMs impractical. They highlight the capabilities of models like Phi-2, which, despite their smaller size, can achieve impressive results in specific AIED tasks like knowledge component (KC) discovery. The paper presents a case study where Phi-2 is used to build a KC discovery algorithm that outperforms both expert-defined KCs and those generated by a larger GPT model.  The authors advocate for more research and development in SLM-based AIED approaches, emphasizing their potential to democratize access to high-quality AI tools in education.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The core idea—emphasizing the importance of SLMs in AIED—is timely and relevant. While the individual components (e.g., using language models for KC discovery) aren't entirely new, the specific combination of *focusing* on SLMs *within the context of AIED's accessibility concerns* and *demonstrating superior performance against LLMs* in a KC discovery task adds novelty. The KC discovery algorithm using Phi-2 as a "probability machine" rather than for direct text generation appears to be a significant contribution.
* **Significance:**  The significance hinges on the potential impact of wider adoption of SLM-based AIED. The paper makes a strong case that LLM reliance creates a digital divide, especially considering the financial and computational resources required. Showcasing a tangible example (KC discovery) where an SLM *outperforms* LLMs challenges the prevailing "bigger is always better" mindset.  If the findings are robust and generalize to other AIED tasks, this could lead to more inclusive AIED practices. The emphasis on data quality as a training approach is also significant.
* **Strengths:**
    * **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the problem of unequal access to AIED tools due to the resource intensity of LLMs.
    * **Well-defined Argument:** The authors present a compelling argument for SLMs as a viable alternative.
    * **Empirical Evidence:** The KC discovery case study provides concrete evidence supporting the claims about SLM capabilities.
    * **Practical Implications:** The paper offers practical implications for AIED researchers and practitioners, encouraging them to explore and develop SLM-based solutions.
    * **Focus on ethical considerations:** The paper rightfully brings in ethical considerations for the future of the field.
* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Limited Scope of Case Study:**  The KC discovery case study, while promising, is a single example. Generalizability to other AIED tasks needs to be established. This represents a strong point for future studies and research.
    * **Definition of SLM:** While the paper provides a useful definition, the rapidly evolving landscape of language models makes any such definition inherently temporary.  It would strengthen the paper to consider a broader range of factors beyond parameter count, such as energy consumption and deployment costs.
    * **Benchmarking:** The paper compared itself to GPT-4o, which isn't publicly available. While impressive, this reduces the impact of the claim due to inaccessibility.
* **Potential Influence:** The paper could shift the AIED community's focus, encouraging researchers to explore SLMs. It could also influence funding decisions, directing resources toward the development of more accessible AIED technologies. The paper could lead to a broader understanding of the trade-offs between model size, performance, and accessibility in AIED.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper presents a timely and important argument for focusing on SLMs in AIED. The KC discovery case study provides solid empirical evidence and demonstrates potential. While further research is needed to validate the generalizability of the findings and update the definition of an SLM, the paper's novelty and potential significance warrant a favorable score. While it has certain limitations in scope, the potential impact of this paper on creating more ethical and accessible AIED solutions is evident.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Enhancing Thyroid Cytology Diagnosis with RAG-Optimized LLMs and Pa-thology Foundation Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.08590v1)
- **Authors**: Hussien Al-Asi, Jordan P Reynolds, Shweta Agarwal, Bryan J Dangott, Aziza Nassar, Zeynettin Akkus
- **Abstract**: Advancements in artificial intelligence (AI) are transforming pathology by integrat-ing large language models (LLMs) with retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) and domain-specific foundation models. This study explores the application of RAG-enhanced LLMs coupled with pathology foundation models for thyroid cytology diagnosis, addressing challenges in cytological interpretation, standardization, and diagnostic accuracy. By leveraging a curated knowledge base, RAG facilitates dy-namic retrieval of relevant case studies, diagnostic criteria, and expert interpreta-tion, improving the contextual understanding of LLMs. Meanwhile, pathology foun-dation models, trained on high-resolution pathology images, refine feature extrac-tion and classification capabilities. The fusion of these AI-driven approaches en-hances diagnostic consistency, reduces variability, and supports pathologists in dis-tinguishing benign from malignant thyroid lesions. Our results demonstrate that integrating RAG with pathology-specific LLMs significantly improves diagnostic efficiency and interpretability, paving the way for AI-assisted thyroid cytopathology, with foundation model UNI achieving AUC 0.73-0.93 for correct prediction of surgi-cal pathology diagnosis from thyroid cytology samples.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the application of Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG)-enhanced Large Language Models (LLMs) coupled with pathology-specific foundation models for improving thyroid cytology diagnosis. The approach aims to address diagnostic challenges like subjective interpretation and inter-observer variability. The RAG system dynamically retrieves relevant case studies, diagnostic criteria, and expert interpretations to improve LLM contextual understanding. Pathology foundation models, trained on high-resolution pathology images, refine feature extraction and classification.  The results show that the combined approach enhances diagnostic consistency, interpretability, and reduces variability. Specifically, the UNI foundation model achieved AUCs ranging from 0.73 to 0.93 for predicting surgical pathology diagnosis from cytology samples.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** While the individual components (LLMs, RAG, pathology foundation models) are not entirely novel, their integration in this specific domain (thyroid cytology diagnosis) and the particular combination of these techniques represents a reasonably novel approach. There's a clear attempt to bridge the gap between advanced language processing and domain-specific image analysis. However, previous works have also explored using LLMs for pathology, making this an incremental advancement rather than a radical breakthrough. The application of *multiple* foundational models (UNI, Virchow, Gigapath, ViT-32) is a positive aspect that could add value if rigorously tested against each other.

*   **Significance:** The potential impact of this research lies in improving diagnostic accuracy, efficiency, and standardization in thyroid cytology. The AUS category in TBSRTC often presents diagnostic challenges, and this AI-driven method could potentially reduce the need for further molecular testing or surgical follow-up. The claimed improvements in diagnostic consistency and interpretability are significant for pathologists. If validated on larger, more diverse datasets, this technology could be translated into a practical clinical tool. However, clinical translation is a significant hurdle that is not demonstrated by the work presented.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The paper clearly articulates the problem and the proposed solution.
    *   The use of RAG enhances the contextual understanding of LLMs, which is a valuable addition.
    *   The evaluation includes multiple foundation models.
    *   The study highlights potential benefits such as improved diagnostic efficiency and reduced diagnostic variability.
    *   The integration of the LLM and pathology pipeline into an existing digital pathology platform ZAPP makes deployment more practical.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Small Dataset:** The biggest limitation is the very small dataset (13 patient records, 36 slides). This significantly limits the generalizability of the results and raises concerns about overfitting.
    *   **Lack of External Validation:** The study lacks external validation on independent datasets.
    *   **Limited Comparison:** While the paper mentions different foundation models, the comparison metrics are limited to Top-1/3/5 accuracy and AUC. A deeper analysis of the specific strengths and weaknesses of each model would have been valuable.
    *   **Unclear RAG Details:** Specifics on the construction of the knowledge base for RAG, the retrieval process, and prompt engineering are somewhat vague. The specific diagnostic and criteria that RAG leverages are not detailed, meaning replication would be difficult.
    *   **Limited Clinical Insight:** A discussion with experienced pathologists on the usability and real-world impact of the results would be a valuable addition.
    * The paper lacks information on how the ROIs were defined and selected, creating a bias.

*   **Potential Influence:** If the findings can be replicated on larger datasets and validated externally, this approach has the potential to influence the workflow in thyroid cytology and assist pathologists in clinical decision-making. However, the current study serves more as a proof-of-concept rather than a definitive solution.

*   **Overall Assessment:** Given the limited dataset, lack of external validation, and the incremental nature of the advancements, the paper doesn't represent a major breakthrough. The small dataset size is a critical limitation that casts significant doubt on the robustness and generalizability of the findings. It provides a solid foundation for future work, but more validation is needed.

Score: 5
Rationale: The paper presents a reasonably novel integration of existing techniques. Its potential significance is clear, but the lack of validation on larger, more diverse datasets is a substantial weakness. The paper reads more like a pilot study or proof-of-concept and has not yet demonstrated its significant added value relative to existing workflows.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 5/10

### Boosting Zero-shot Stereo Matching using Large-scale Mixed Images Sources in the Real World
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.08607v1)
- **Authors**: Yuran Wang, Yingping Liang, Ying Fu
- **Abstract**: Stereo matching methods rely on dense pixel-wise ground truth labels, which are laborious to obtain, especially for real-world datasets. The scarcity of labeled data and domain gaps between synthetic and real-world images also pose notable challenges. In this paper, we propose a novel framework, \textbf{BooSTer}, that leverages both vision foundation models and large-scale mixed image sources, including synthetic, real, and single-view images. First, to fully unleash the potential of large-scale single-view images, we design a data generation strategy combining monocular depth estimation and diffusion models to generate dense stereo matching data from single-view images. Second, to tackle sparse labels in real-world datasets, we transfer knowledge from monocular depth estimation models, using pseudo-mono depth labels and a dynamic scale- and shift-invariant loss for additional supervision. Furthermore, we incorporate vision foundation model as an encoder to extract robust and transferable features, boosting accuracy and generalization. Extensive experiments on benchmark datasets demonstrate the effectiveness of our approach, achieving significant improvements in accuracy over existing methods, particularly in scenarios with limited labeled data and domain shifts.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces BooSTer, a novel framework for zero-shot stereo matching that leverages vision foundation models (VFMs) and large-scale mixed image sources (synthetic, real-world, single-view) to address limitations in current stereo matching methods. The core components include: (1) a monocular-depth-guided stereo data generation pipeline to expand training data from single-view images using depth estimation and diffusion models, (2) a dynamic scale- and shift-invariant loss to transfer knowledge from monocular depth models to handle sparse labels in real-world datasets, and (3) a hybrid encoder integrating a VFM (DINOv2) with a CNN to extract robust and transferable features.  Experiments on benchmark datasets demonstrate improved accuracy and generalization, especially in scenarios with limited labeled data and domain shifts.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Addressing a Relevant Problem:** The paper tackles the significant challenges of limited labeled data and domain gaps in stereo matching, which are practical concerns for deploying such systems in real-world applications.
*   **Innovative Use of VFMs:**  Integrating VFMs like DINOv2 into the stereo matching pipeline is a novel approach. VFMs offer pre-trained semantic and feature extraction capabilities, which are underexplored in traditional stereo matching architectures. The hybrid encoder design effectively combines the global context captured by VFMs with local details extracted by CNNs.
*   **Effective Data Augmentation:**  The monocular-depth-guided stereo data generation pipeline is well-designed and addresses the limitation of synthetic data by incorporating real-world information via depth estimation and diffusion models.  The edge-aware inpainting module improves the realism of generated images.
*   **Dynamic Scale- and Shift-Invariant Loss:** The DSSI loss is a clever way to leverage information from monocular depth estimation when ground truth data is sparse, enabling the transfer of relative depth information effectively.
*   **Strong Experimental Results:**  The experimental results across multiple datasets (KITTI, ETH3D, Middlebury) demonstrate a significant improvement in zero-shot performance compared to existing methods, validating the efficacy of the proposed approach. The ablation studies systematically justify the design choices of the framework.
*   **Clear Writing and Structure:** The paper is well-written and organized, clearly explaining the proposed methodology and experimental setup.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Complexity:**  The framework incorporates several components (VFM, diffusion models, DSSI loss), potentially increasing the complexity of implementation and training compared to simpler approaches.
*   **Computational Cost:** Using VFMs like DINOv2 can increase the computational cost of the encoder, which can be a limitation for real-time applications, this is not fully discussed in the paper.
*   **Incremental Improvement:** While the results demonstrate improvements, the magnitude of improvement, in some cases, is incremental rather than revolutionary. Also, the study focuses primarily on demonstrating the framework and doesn't dive deeply into exploring alternative VFMs or data generation strategies in more detail.
*   **Limited Ablation:** There is a lack of comparison between different VFM models (like CLIP vs. DINOv2) in feature extraction.
*   **Data Sampling Concerns**: While the large-scale mixed dataset shows a boost, a more detailed discussion of the rationale behind the specific data sampling frequencies (5:6:1) for the different datasets would add more rigor.

**Novelty and Significance:**

The paper is novel in its integration of VFMs and mixed data sources for zero-shot stereo matching. The specific combination of monocular-depth-guided data generation, the DSSI loss, and the hybrid VFM-CNN encoder is a significant contribution. The results show a tangible improvement in generalization performance, which is crucial for practical applications. While the individual components may not be entirely new, their synergistic combination within this framework constitutes a meaningful advance. The framework’s emphasis on leveraging readily available, but previously underutilized, data sources makes it practical and impactful.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a significant contribution to the field of stereo matching by addressing the crucial problem of generalization. While the individual components of the method are not groundbreaking on their own, the way they are combined and the effective use of VFMs to achieve better zero-shot performance is indeed a novel contribution. The method is well-validated with experiments and demonstrates clear benefits.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### WaveGuard: Robust Deepfake Detection and Source Tracing via Dual-Tree Complex Wavelet and Graph Neural Networks
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.08614v1)
- **Authors**: Ziyuan He, Zhiqing Guo, Liejun Wang, Gaobo Yang, Yunfeng Diao, Dan Ma
- **Abstract**: Deepfake technology poses increasing risks such as privacy invasion and identity theft. To address these threats, we propose WaveGuard, a proactive watermarking framework that enhances robustness and imperceptibility via frequency-domain embedding and graph-based structural consistency. Specifically, we embed watermarks into high-frequency sub-bands using Dual-Tree Complex Wavelet Transform (DT-CWT) and employ a Structural Consistency Graph Neural Network (SC-GNN) to preserve visual quality. We also design an attention module to refine embedding precision. Experimental results on face swap and reenactment tasks demonstrate that WaveGuard outperforms state-of-the-art methods in both robustness and visual quality. Code is available at https://github.com/vpsg-research/WaveGuard.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces WaveGuard, a novel proactive watermarking framework designed to improve the robustness and imperceptibility of deepfake detection and source tracing. WaveGuard leverages a dual-channel approach, combining frequency-domain embedding using Dual-Tree Complex Wavelet Transform (DT-CWT) and structural consistency optimization using a Graph Neural Network (GNN).  The watermark is embedded in high-frequency sub-bands derived from DT-CWT to enhance robustness against distortions and deepfake forgeries. The GNN, dubbed SC-GNN, extracts structural features from both original and watermarked images to ensure consistency and minimize perceptual artifacts.  A Tanh-based Spatial Embedding Attention Module (TSEAM) is also implemented to further refine features and improve visual quality. The authors present experimental results demonstrating WaveGuard's superior performance in face swap and face replay tasks compared to existing methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper possesses a reasonable degree of novelty by combining several established techniques in a new way. Specifically:
    * **DT-CWT for Watermarking:** Using DT-CWT for robust watermarking, especially in the context of deepfake countermeasures, is a strong point.  The shift-invariance and directional selectivity of DT-CWT make it a good choice for this application.
    * **SC-GNN for Invisibility:**  The application of a graph neural network to enforce structural consistency between original and watermarked images is a novel approach to minimize visual artifacts. While GNNs have been used in image processing before, this specific application for watermarking is interesting.
    * **Joint Sub-band correlation** leveraging for watermark propagation and extraction.

* **Significance:** The paper addresses a very important problem: the increasing prevalence of deepfakes and the need for robust and traceable detection mechanisms. A significant weakness is that there is limited innovation within the components themselves, this is more of an integration of various existing techniques. While proactive watermarking is a hot area, there is significant research already in this direction. Another potential weakness is reliance on publicly available pre-trained datasets to verify and test the effectiveness of the proposed model.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Robustness:** The frequency-domain embedding strategy seems promising for resisting common distortions and deepfake attacks.
    * **Invisibility:**  The SC-GNN and TSEAM are effective in minimizing visual artifacts, a critical requirement for proactive watermarking.
    * **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The authors provide a detailed experimental setup, including comparisons with relevant baselines and ablation studies.
    * **Well written and clear explanations** make the information readily accessible.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Incremental Novelty:** While the combination is novel, each individual component (DT-CWT, GNNs, attention mechanisms) is well-established.  The paper's contribution is primarily in the integration rather than fundamental algorithmic breakthroughs.
    * **Limited Dataset Diversity:**  Relying heavily on CelebA-HQ for training and evaluation might limit the generalization ability of WaveGuard to other types of images or videos. Testing performance on low-quality, compressed, or noisy images needs to be addressed.
    * **Semi Robust Detection** The proposed model provides limited security guarantees when faced with malicious attacks and requires a manual selection of parameters to perform properly under such cases, which poses as a limitation.

* **Potential Impact:** If the method proves to be truly robust and generalizable, it could be a valuable tool for combating deepfakes and protecting media authenticity. However, further investigation is needed to assess its vulnerability to advanced deepfake generation techniques and adaptive attacks specifically designed to remove or circumvent the watermark. The code availability will facilitate further research and validation.

**Score:** 6.5

**Rationale:**

WaveGuard presents a worthwhile contribution to the field of deepfake detection by effectively combining existing techniques to create a robust and imperceptible watermarking system. While it does not introduce groundbreaking new algorithms, the careful integration of DT-CWT, SC-GNN, and attention mechanisms demonstrates a practical approach to addressing the challenges of proactive watermarking. The weaknesses, specifically the incremental novelty and dataset limitations, hinder the paper from achieving a higher score. The potential for impact is contingent on the results of extensive validation and independent verification of robustness.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### Resource-Efficient Language Models: Quantization for Fast and Accessible Inference
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.08620v1)
- **Authors**: Tollef Emil Jørgensen
- **Abstract**: Large language models have significantly advanced natural language processing, yet their heavy resource demands pose severe challenges regarding hardware accessibility and energy consumption. This paper presents a focused and high-level review of post-training quantization (PTQ) techniques designed to optimize the inference efficiency of LLMs by the end-user, including details on various quantization schemes, granularities, and trade-offs. The aim is to provide a balanced overview between the theory and applications of post-training quantization.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a concise summary and a critical evaluation of the paper, including novelty, significance, and a score with justification.

**Summary:**

The paper "Resource-Efficient Language Models: Quantization for Fast and Accessible Inference" presents a high-level review of post-training quantization (PTQ) techniques aimed at optimizing the inference efficiency of large language models (LLMs). It covers various quantization schemes, granularities, and trade-offs, providing a balanced overview of both the theory and practical applications of PTQ. The paper emphasizes the importance of PTQ in addressing the resource demands of LLMs, making them more accessible for deployment in environments with limited computational resources. It also covers challenges such as dealing with outliers and different granularities of quantization. Finally, the paper discusses several PTQ methods and libraries, including ZeroQuant, LLM.int8(), GPTQ, AWQ, SmoothQuant, and HQQ, outlining their availability and features.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper doesn't present novel research or a new quantization technique. It positions itself as a review or overview, which automatically reduces its inherent novelty. However, its novelty lies in its cohesive and focused presentation of the topic for the *end-user*, rather than the researcher focused on algorithms or alternative data types. The paper is not groundbreaking in the sense of introducing new algorithms or frameworks. It mostly compiles existing knowledge.

*   **Significance:** The significance is more prominent, and it stems from several factors:

    *   **Accessibility:** The paper aims to provide a more accessible introduction to PTQ for individuals looking to apply it to existing LLMs, rather than delving into the theoretical complexities. This addresses a practical need in the community.
    *   **Relevance:** The resource demands of LLMs are a pressing issue, limiting their widespread adoption. The paper addresses a crucial area for democratizing access to these models.
    *   **Timeliness:** Given the rapid advancements in quantization, a focused review that synthesizes recent methods and tools can be valuable.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Organization:** The paper is well-structured, starting with background information, then delving into various methods and their implementation details.
    *   **Practical Focus:** The emphasis on post-training quantization and its applicability to common LLMs makes it useful for practitioners.
    *   **Comprehensive Overview:** The paper covers a range of PTQ methods and mentions key libraries, making it a good starting point for researchers or developers seeking to implement PTQ.
    *   **Helpful illustrations**: Provides understandable illustrations of how values shift during quantization, and distributions during outlier handling.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Lack of Original Research:** The paper does not contain any new experimental results or a novel analysis. It's largely a compilation of existing knowledge.
    *   **Depth of Analysis:** While providing an overview, the paper could benefit from a deeper comparative analysis of the different PTQ methods in terms of their accuracy, speed, and memory footprint.
    *   **Limited Scope:** Focus is primarily end-user.
    *   **Rapid Obsolescence**: Quantization evolves quickly. As such, any survey is immediately at risk of becoming partially outdated.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper can serve as a valuable starting point for researchers and practitioners entering the field of LLM quantization, providing a clear and accessible overview of the available techniques and tools. It may indirectly influence the adoption of PTQ methods and contribute to making LLMs more accessible.

**Overall Assessment:**

The paper's value resides in its role as a practical and focused introduction to PTQ, especially targeted at end-users wanting to make use of efficient quantized models. While it lacks original research or in-depth comparative analysis, it provides a valuable service in synthesizing and presenting information in a clear and accessible manner.

**Score: 6**

**Rationale:** The score reflects the paper's significance as a valuable overview of a rapidly evolving field. While it lacks novel research contributions (preventing a higher score), its accessibility and practical focus warrant a rating above average. However, some missing technical detail, analysis and rapid obsolescence prevent a higher rating.

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 6/10

### Visually Guided Decoding: Gradient-Free Hard Prompt Inversion with Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.08622v1)
- **Authors**: Donghoon Kim, Minji Bae, Kyuhong Shim, Byonghyo Shim
- **Abstract**: Text-to-image generative models like DALL-E and Stable Diffusion have revolutionized visual content creation across various applications, including advertising, personalized media, and design prototyping. However, crafting effective textual prompts to guide these models remains challenging, often requiring extensive trial and error. Existing prompt inversion approaches, such as soft and hard prompt techniques, are not so effective due to the limited interpretability and incoherent prompt generation. To address these issues, we propose Visually Guided Decoding (VGD), a gradient-free approach that leverages large language models (LLMs) and CLIP-based guidance to generate coherent and semantically aligned prompts. In essence, VGD utilizes the robust text generation capabilities of LLMs to produce human-readable prompts. Further, by employing CLIP scores to ensure alignment with user-specified visual concepts, VGD enhances the interpretability, generalization, and flexibility of prompt generation without the need for additional training. Our experiments demonstrate that VGD outperforms existing prompt inversion techniques in generating understandable and contextually relevant prompts, facilitating more intuitive and controllable interactions with text-to-image models.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Visually Guided Decoding (VGD), a novel gradient-free approach for prompt inversion in text-to-image generation.  VGD leverages large language models (LLMs) and CLIP-based guidance to generate coherent and semantically aligned prompts from a given image. The core idea is to use the strong text generation capabilities of LLMs to produce human-readable prompts and CLIP to ensure alignment with visual concepts from the input image.  The authors demonstrate that VGD outperforms existing prompt inversion techniques by producing more understandable, generalizable, and flexible prompts, leading to more intuitive and controllable interactions with text-to-image models.  They showcase applications like style transfer, image editing, and multi-concept image generation using VGD. The approach does not require additional training, making it easily integrable with chat-based interfaces.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*Novelty:* The core idea of using an LLM *guided* by CLIP to perform prompt inversion is moderately novel.  Prior work has explored LLMs for image captioning and prompt generation, and CLIP has been widely used for aligning text and images.  The specific *combination* of these components in a *gradient-free* inversion framework appears to be a worthwhile contribution.  The "gradient-free" aspect is important, as it allows leveraging pretrained LLMs without requiring computationally expensive fine-tuning. However, similar to CLIP Interrogator, the approach fundamentally relies on existing models and does not introduce new architectural components or loss functions.

*Significance:* The significance of the paper lies primarily in its practical benefits:

*   **Interpretability:**  The generated prompts are human-readable, which is a significant advantage over "soft prompt" methods that produce non-interpretable embedding vectors. This enables users to understand and modify the prompts for better control over image generation.
*   **Generalizability:** The paper shows that VGD-generated prompts work across different text-to-image models (Stable Diffusion, Midjourney, DALL-E 2) and datasets.  This is crucial for practical adoption.
*   **Flexibility:**  The ability to easily perform style transfer, image editing, and multi-concept generation using modified VGD prompts is a strong selling point.
*   **Ease of Use:** Since it is gradient-free, the approach is computationally lightweight.

*Strengths:*

*   Clear and well-written presentation.
*   Comprehensive experimental evaluation including quantitative (CLIP-I score, BERTScore) and qualitative results.
*   Ablation studies demonstrating the importance of both the LLM and CLIP components.
*   Demonstration of various applications.

*Weaknesses:*

*   The method still struggles with fine-grained details and complex scenes containing multiple objects, particularly regional objects in the background. This limitation is acknowledged by the authors.
*   The reliance on CLIP can be a bottleneck if the CLIP model is not well-aligned with the text-to-image model being used. The authors show this through ablation studies.
*   The paper does not offer a completely radical change in the field. It mainly introduces a new method for generating prompts that are more controllable and interpretable.

*Potential Impact:*

The work has good potential to be influential because it offers a practical and easy-to-use solution for generating effective prompts, which can greatly improve the accessibility and usability of text-to-image models.  It could potentially replace more complicated and less-transparent techniques currently in use.  The method could also be further explored in other generative tasks, such as text generation or audio generation.

**Justification for the Score:**

While the paper doesn't introduce groundbreaking theoretical insights or novel architectures, the practicality and effectiveness of the VGD approach, combined with its clear advantages over existing prompt inversion techniques, make it a valuable contribution. The interpretability aspect, in particular, will likely resonate with practitioners and users. The well-executed experiments provide a convincing case for the method's performance. However, the limitations concerning handling fine-grained details and complex scenes, and the reliance on CLIP alignment, prevent it from achieving a higher score. A score of 7 reflects the balance between these factors.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### Revealing economic facts: LLMs know more than they say
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.08662v1)
- **Authors**: Marcus Buckmann, Quynh Anh Nguyen, Edward Hill
- **Abstract**: We investigate whether the hidden states of large language models (LLMs) can be used to estimate and impute economic and financial statistics. Focusing on county-level (e.g. unemployment) and firm-level (e.g. total assets) variables, we show that a simple linear model trained on the hidden states of open-source LLMs outperforms the models' text outputs. This suggests that hidden states capture richer economic information than the responses of the LLMs reveal directly. A learning curve analysis indicates that only a few dozen labelled examples are sufficient for training. We also propose a transfer learning method that improves estimation accuracy without requiring any labelled data for the target variable. Finally, we demonstrate the practical utility of hidden-state representations in super-resolution and data imputation tasks.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper explores the use of large language models (LLMs) to estimate and impute economic and financial statistics. The authors investigate whether the hidden states (embeddings) of LLMs contain useful information beyond what is explicitly stated in their text outputs. They find that a simple linear model trained on these hidden states often outperforms the model's direct text predictions, particularly for less common economic indicators. The study further proposes transfer learning methods to improve estimation accuracy, even without labeled data for the target variable. Finally, it demonstrates the application of hidden-state representations in tasks like super-resolution (estimating data at finer granularities) and data imputation, indicating potential practical utility.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The central idea – leveraging the hidden states of LLMs for economic data analysis – possesses a moderate degree of novelty.  While the general concept of probing LLM embeddings isn't entirely new, its specific application to *economic and financial data imputation and estimation* is a distinct contribution.  The transfer learning methods and demonstrations for super-resolution are incremental innovations, building on existing techniques, but adapted to the specifics of LLM hidden state representations.  Prior works have explored LLM recall of geospatial statistics. The focus here is less about direct recall and more about *inference* of statistics via hidden representations and the task-specific applications.

**Significance:** The potential impact of this work is noteworthy, but the significance depends on the real-world reliability and scalability of the findings.  If LLMs can indeed augment or even partially replace traditional data sources, it could reduce reliance on expensive and time-consuming data collection/cleaning processes in economic modeling and forecasting.  The finding that a small amount of labeled data is enough for the linear model is particularly practically significant. However, the paper needs to address concerns of consistency and biases arising from the LLM training data.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly frames the problem of reducing dependence on traditional data sources and explores LLMs as a potential solution.
*   **Empirical Validation:** The authors provide extensive empirical analyses across various datasets (US, UK, EU, Germany) and LLM architectures (Llama 3 sizes, Phi-3). This strengthens the credibility of their findings.
*   **Practical Applications:** The demonstrations of super-resolution and imputation provide concrete examples of how these techniques could be applied.
*   **Transfer learning results:**  demonstration of transfer learning with no labels on the target variable is an important finding.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **LLM Bias and Consistency:** The paper needs to address the potential biases embedded in the LLMs' training data and outputs. LLMs can perpetuate or amplify existing societal inequalities, and it's crucial to acknowledge this risk when using them for economic analysis, where equitable outcomes are often paramount. Also, LLM outputs can be inconsistent, which can affect data integrity when used for estimation. How sensitive are the LME results to variation in the hidden states from different LLM generations (e.g., Llama 3 versions)?
*   **Scalability:** While the authors use open-source LLMs, the computational cost of extracting and processing embeddings for large datasets could be a limitation. Scalability considerations are important.
*   **Model Selection:** The linear model applied to the hidden states is a relatively simple approach. While its simplicity is an advantage, are there more sophisticated models (e.g., neural networks) that could extract even more information? The paper should discuss why this choice was made (e.g., computational efficiency, interpretability).
*   **Performance Limitations:** While the LME beats the text output, the performance may not be high enough for deployment. More analysis is needed.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper has merit due to its novel application of LLM hidden states to a relevant problem (economic data analysis), supported by empirical evidence. However, the novelty is incremental rather than groundbreaking, and several limitations (bias, scalability, model selection) need to be addressed more thoroughly. The significance hinges on the reliability and practicality of the findings. More rigorous investigation is required.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### A Social Robot with Inner Speech for Dietary Guidance
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.08664v1)
- **Authors**: Valerio Belcamino, Alessandro Carfì, Valeria Seidita, Fulvio Mastrogiovanni, Antonio Chella
- **Abstract**: We explore the use of inner speech as a mechanism to enhance transparency and trust in social robots for dietary advice. In humans, inner speech structures thought processes and decision-making; in robotics, it improves explainability by making reasoning explicit. This is crucial in healthcare scenarios, where trust in robotic assistants depends on both accurate recommendations and human-like dialogue, which make interactions more natural and engaging. Building on this, we developed a social robot that provides dietary advice, and we provided the architecture with inner speech capabilities to validate user input, refine reasoning, and generate clear justifications. The system integrates large language models for natural language understanding and a knowledge graph for structured dietary information. By making decisions more transparent, our approach strengthens trust and improves human-robot interaction in healthcare. We validated this by measuring the computational efficiency of our architecture and conducting a small user study, which assessed the reliability of inner speech in explaining the robot's behavior.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper explores using "inner speech" as a method to enhance transparency and trust in social robots, especially in dietary advice applications. The authors propose a cognitive architecture that integrates an inner speech module into a social robot. This module uses large language models (LLMs) to generate a natural language self-dialogue during decision-making. By externalizing (selectively sharing) this inner speech, the robot provides users with insights into its reasoning process. The system uses a knowledge graph for dietary information and symbolic reasoning alongside LLMs. The authors present a prototype implementation and evaluate its computational efficiency and user understanding through a small user study. The results suggest that inner speech improves transparency and understanding without significantly increasing latency.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of incorporating inner speech (or self-talk) into robots to improve explainability is not entirely new, as the authors acknowledge their previous work [24]. However, the combination of this concept with LLMs, a knowledge graph, and symbolic reasoning for dietary advice is a novel integration. The explicit focus on trust and transparency in the sensitive domain of healthcare further adds to its novelty.

*   **Significance:**  The paper tackles a critical challenge in HRI: building trust in robotic assistants, especially in high-stakes applications. Explainability is crucial for fostering trust and user adoption. The research contributes to the understanding of how robots can communicate their reasoning process effectively to humans.  If successfully implemented and scaled, this approach could lead to more effective and trustworthy robotic assistants in healthcare and other domains.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper presents a clear and well-structured cognitive architecture.
    *   The integration of LLMs with symbolic reasoning addresses the limitations of LLMs ("stochastic parrots") and enhances decision-making reliability.
    *   The timing analysis provides valuable insights into the computational costs of the different modules.
    *   The user study demonstrates the potential of inner speech to improve user understanding.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The user study is small (14 participants) and conducted in Italian, limiting the generalizability of the findings. Further study is needed with a larger and more diverse population.
    *   While timing analyses are provided, the overall response times remain relatively high, especially when replanning is required.  This may hinder fluid, natural interactions.
    *   The paper relies heavily on Groq's free-tier APIs for LLM inference. The performance and cost-effectiveness of this approach may not scale well to real-world deployments.
    * The prompts used for LLMs were not provided. More detail in the method will allow replication or comparison of the approach.
    *  Limited discussion on failure cases.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper could inspire further research on:
    *   Developing more sophisticated inner speech mechanisms for robots.
    *   Investigating the impact of inner speech on user trust, engagement, and compliance in various domains.
    *   Exploring alternative methods for balancing the flexibility of LLMs with the reliability of symbolic reasoning.
    *   Improving the efficiency and scalability of LLM-based robotic systems.

**Justification for Score:**

While the paper builds upon existing research and has limitations in its experimental evaluation, it presents a novel integration of techniques and addresses a significant challenge in HRI. The focus on trust and transparency in healthcare is particularly relevant. The modular architecture and the timing analysis provide valuable insights for future research and development. However, the small user study, language limitation, relatively high response times, and reliance on a potentially unsustainable LLM API limit the impact of the paper.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.RO
- **Score**: 7/10

### A Mamba-based Network for Semi-supervised Singing Melody Extraction Using Confidence Binary Regularization
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.08681v1)
- **Authors**: Xiaoliang He, Kangjie Dong, Jingkai Cao, Shuai Yu, Wei Li, Yi Yu
- **Abstract**: Singing melody extraction (SME) is a key task in the field of music information retrieval. However, existing methods are facing several limitations: firstly, prior models use transformers to capture the contextual dependencies, which requires quadratic computation resulting in low efficiency in the inference stage. Secondly, prior works typically rely on frequencysupervised methods to estimate the fundamental frequency (f0), which ignores that the musical performance is actually based on notes. Thirdly, transformers typically require large amounts of labeled data to achieve optimal performances, but the SME task lacks of sufficient annotated data. To address these issues, in this paper, we propose a mamba-based network, called SpectMamba, for semi-supervised singing melody extraction using confidence binary regularization. In particular, we begin by introducing vision mamba to achieve computational linear complexity. Then, we propose a novel note-f0 decoder that allows the model to better mimic the musical performance. Further, to alleviate the scarcity of the labeled data, we introduce a confidence binary regularization (CBR) module to leverage the unlabeled data by maximizing the probability of the correct classes. The proposed method is evaluated on several public datasets and the conducted experiments demonstrate the effectiveness of our proposed method.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces SpectMamba, a Mamba-based network for semi-supervised singing melody extraction (SME).  It addresses limitations in existing transformer-based methods, such as high computational complexity and reliance on frequency-supervised approaches that ignore the musical structure of notes.  The proposed SpectMamba architecture utilizes a vision mamba-inspired encoder for efficient processing, a note-f0 decoder to simulate musical performance using a coarse-to-fine approach (note sequence estimation followed by fine-grained f0 estimation), and a confidence binary regularization (CBR) module to leverage unlabeled data and encourage consistent classifications across augmented versions of the input.  Experiments on standard datasets demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed method, showing improvements over existing approaches in terms of both performance and efficiency.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents several novel aspects.  First, the application of Mamba-based networks to SME is itself a novel contribution, potentially offering a more efficient alternative to transformers. The note-f0 decoder using a coarse-to-fine note-aware approach seems like a reasonable and potentially effective way to incorporate musical knowledge. Furthermore, the CBR module for semi-supervised learning is a novel attempt to leverage unlabeled data in the context of SME. However, the individual components are inspired by techniques from other domains (vision Mamba, AllMatch), so the novelty lies in their *combination* and adaptation to the SME task.

*   **Significance:** The potential significance of the paper lies in improving the efficiency and accuracy of SME. The reduced computational complexity of Mamba-based networks makes the approach more practical for real-time applications and deployment on resource-constrained devices. The semi-supervised approach also is important as the creation of perfectly labelled music data is an expensive and time consuming task. The paper achieves promising results compared to existing techniques which may inspire more works in this direction.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Efficient Architecture:** The use of Mamba reduces the computational complexity compared to transformer-based models.
    *   **Note-Aware f0 Estimation:** The note-f0 decoder attempts to incorporate musical structure into the f0 estimation process, which aligns with the nature of music.
    *   **Semi-Supervised Learning:** The CBR module addresses the issue of limited labeled data, which is a common problem in SME.
    *   **Strong Experimental Results:** The paper demonstrates performance improvements over several baselines on multiple standard datasets.
    *   **Clear and Well-Structured Paper:** The paper is well-organized and clearly explains the proposed method and experimental results.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Incremental Novelty:** As noted above, the components are largely adapted from other fields. The real contribution lies in the synergistic combination to solve the SME task.
    *   **Ablation Details:** While there's an ablation study, it could be more comprehensive.  For instance, exploring different configurations of the CBR module could be valuable.
    *   **Limited Unlabeled Data Exploration:** While using unlabeled data improves performance, the full potential of semi-supervised learning could be further investigated with more diverse and larger unlabeled datasets.
    *   **Dataset Bias:** The paper only uses three datasets. It will be better to conduct the experiments on more datasets.

*   **Justification:**

SpectMamba addresses the important practical concern of computational efficiency in singing melody extraction (SME), presenting a potentially significant contribution to the field. It is also the first one to combine vision Mamba, note-aware melody extraction, and semi-supervised learning for melody extraction. However, the individual novelty of each component is limited and it's unclear how each component is individually contributing to performance gains.
